# Independent material review: LLP 3000.036.001 PAGE5 — round 2

Return exactly `VERDICT: READY` on the first line if this scoped revision has no material correctness, authorization soundness, compatibility, model-agreement, or coverage defect. Otherwise return exactly `VERDICT: NOT READY` on the first line and list only material actionable findings with committed file and line references. Do not edit files. Treat this as a fresh independent review; do not consult `llp/reviews/`, another reviewer, or uncommitted working-tree changes. Do not defer to tests.

Review exactly these four PAGE5 commits in `/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang`:

- implementation and governing LLP amendment: `4dbf2ffc8e`
- formal, differential, simulator, device, and cut coverage: `c1e494491b`
- Lean required-column correction: `f3e35c177b`
- review corrections and model alignment: `5447e593d0`

The shared worktree has extensive unrelated uncommitted and interleaved Wave 12 work. Ignore it. Read exact committed patches with `git show --format=fuller --stat --patch <commit>` and final committed files with `git show 5447e593d0:<path>`. Reviews bind these four commits, not the moving working tree or HEAD. The governing amendment is LLP 3000.036.001 §18 at `5447e593d0`; check it against the implementation and pre-existing PAGE/PAGE4 rules in §§14–17.

Round 1 found four material issues. Commit `5447e593d0` is intended to correct all four; independently verify the corrections rather than assuming the disposition:

1. The core validator previously checked fixed-probe readability before substituting page pins. It now matches the authored rule/key exactly but proves ordinary caller visibility from the generated substituted point.
2. Nested fixed conjunctions previously depended on grouping. It now recursively proves every fixed conjunct (or one fixed disjunct), recognizes only the exact short-circuit `a and b` IR block, and supports one varying membership plus one or more fixed guards.
3. Lean previously accepted exactly two non-pin atoms. It now requires exactly one varying witness and every other atom to be an admissible fixed point.
4. Equality advice previously replaced the first matching index spelling in the query. It now locates the focal authored read through the source map and replaces that span; a regression places an earlier unrelated read with the same index name and recompiles the printed full query plus declaration.

A provisional 2,000-case Lean campaign then found that two independent query-filter `or` conjuncts made `alts=None`, after which Lean's membership classifier silently used an empty alternative. Native correctly stayed incomplete. The model now requires either no query-filter alternative or the one supported distributable alternative for cases 2 and 3. The LLP states this boundary, an executable Lean example returns `complete:false`, and a generated four-seed native/Lean regression checks the shape.

## Required behavior

1. Bounded positive authorization products retain every selected predicate and the original query predicate, and stay within eight descriptors including system. Overflow, negative/fallible alternatives, a residual second varying witness, or membership pages with two undistributed query-filter alternatives retain the honest incomplete fallback.

2. One varying exact membership may coexist with one or more fixed `exists` guards independent of conjunction grouping. Every fixed probe must become a required total unique plain point after exact page-pin substitution, remain timeless and without read-through, and be caller-readable at the generated point. The residual filter must be a necessary conjunction matching the authored rule and pins. Hidden, expiring, non-total, non-unique, extra-equality, malformed Boolean-block, and two-varying forms must not produce a complete merge.

3. Exact vacancy remains limited to a top-level `not exists target[key = .id]` over one required unique plain point on an online-only, unfiltered, unpinned outer page. The private full outer walk evaluates the entire authored rule per row, exposes only authorized outer rows, and reports incomplete on examined or rule-probe cuts. Hidden/timed vacancy targets are permitted because their authored system probe remains private. Synced outer, filtered, compound/non-id/non-unique, or shortcut forms fall back.

4. Equality-filter advice appears only when independent exact equalities establish all missing rule fields. It prints a collision-free declaration on the actual table and the complete query with only the focal scan changed, including when an earlier scan spells the same index. The printed pair recompiles.

5. `branch-pages-v4` fences fixed-residual and vacancy interpretations before old-device execution. Bounded products reuse the existing validator/capability.

6. Lean, generated differential, simulator, and device evidence exercises the actual supported predicates. Unsupported or ordinary-incomplete forms are not counted as successful full-oracle executions.

## High-risk review questions

- Does the new core short-circuit block recognizer accept only conjunction semantics? Can an `or`, assignment mismatch, extra statement, or filter containing fewer necessary guards fool it?
- Does recursive fixed-rule proof require every grouped conjunct, correctly allow one true disjunct, and remain tied to the exact authored residual reads?
- Does point visibility use the generated key without trusting binder metadata, while preserving hidden/timed/read-through refusals?
- Can source-map span arithmetic select the wrong declaration/read for multibyte text, a repeated index spelling, a grouped read, or a generated site?
- Does the generalized Lean fixed classifier agree with native on zero, one, and multiple fixed guards and exactly one varying witness?
- Does the new filter-alternative condition close the campaign finding without rejecting native-supported compound authorization products?
- Do the new multi-fixed generator, simulator, device and Lean cases really execute grouped guards rather than only rename the old case?

+
+## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies
+
+Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
+planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
+argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
+query plan.
+
+**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
+alternatives across conjunctions before it classifies the resulting lanes.
+For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
+alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
+the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
+inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
+negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
+analyzer-limitation fallback.
+
+**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
+be accompanied by fixed `exists` guards. A fixed guard is admissible only when
+the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
+required, total, unique point that is readable under the target's system
+rule. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
+`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
+into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
+the core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
+and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
+authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
+guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
+fragment.
+
+**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
+`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
+plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
+same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
+candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
+authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
+therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
+outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and hidden/timed
+interpretations outside the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the
+fallback. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a
+private target's absence is globally observable.
+
+**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
+independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
+using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
+rewritten query using that same index. The binder recompiles that repair in
+tests. When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
+does not offer this rewrite.
+
+**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences bounded conjunctive
+products, fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device that
+only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
+executing it; existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean
+surface classifier mirrors the exact vacancy admission predicate and the
+private authorization walk. Generated differential cases exercise the new
+lane families on native, simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the
+fragment remain honest analyzer limitations rather than completeness claims.

+/// Exhaustion is observable only when the authored read rule itself contains
+/// a top-level absence check on an exact required point keyed by this row's
+/// intrinsic id. The lane stays private and evaluates the complete rule.
+fn vacancy_branch(schema: &Schema, lane: &crate::ir::MergeLane) -> bool {
+    if lane.source.is_some() || lane.scan.filter.is_some() || !lane.scan.prefix.is_empty() { return false; }
+    let Some(outer) = schema.table(&lane.scan.table) else { return false };
+    if outer.sync.is_some() { return false; }
+    outer.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|rule| {
+        let RuleExpr::Not(inner) = rule else { return false };
+        let RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } = inner.as_ref() else { return false };
+        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
+        let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
+        equalities.is_empty() && key == &[RuleTerm::Field("id".into())]
+            && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.len() == 1
+            && point.components[0].column().is_some_and(|column|
+                target.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
+    })
+}

+/// A generated membership lane may retain a second, prefix-fixed point grant
+/// as a residual predicate.  Recognize only the exact bound point expression:
+/// this proves that every row emitted by the lane satisfies that conjunct,
+/// while a missing, reordered or merely similar probe keeps the merge out.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — one fixed grant may guard one varying per-row grant.
+fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
+    pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
+    if let Expr::Binary { op: crate::ir::BinaryOp::And, left, right } = filter {
+        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
+            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    }
+    let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
+    let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
+    let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
+    equalities.is_empty() && target.through.is_none() && target.not_before.is_none() && !target.is_expiring()
+        && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.iter().all(|part| part.column()
+            .and_then(|column| target.columns.get(column)).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
+        && schema.rule_input_is_readable(table, index, key)
+        && &read.table == table && &read.index == index && read.key.len() == key.len()
+        && read.key.iter().zip(key).all(|(expression, term)| rule_term_expression(term, expression, pins))
+}
+
+fn rule_term_expression(term: &RuleTerm, expression: &Expr, pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
+    match (term, expression) {
+        (RuleTerm::Viewer, Expr::Input(Input::Viewer)) => true,
+        (RuleTerm::Literal(value), Expr::Literal(actual)) => value == actual,
+        (RuleTerm::Field(field), expression) => pins.iter().find_map(|(name, value)|
+            (name.as_str() == field).then_some(*value)).is_some_and(|value| value == expression),
+        _ => false,
+    }
 }

+/// A top-level `not exists point[.id]` explicitly makes vacancy observable.
+/// One server lane checks the original rule for every candidate and may report
+/// exhaustion only after the physical range ends. It carries no ordinary
+/// filter because rule probes use wider system visibility.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact vacancy is the sole non-uniform exhaustion lane.
+pub(super) fn vacancy(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests,
+    read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
+    let range = read.range.as_ref()?;
+    let table = tables.get(&read.table)?;
+    let rule = branches::read_rule(table)?;
+    if read.filter.is_some() || read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some()
+        || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some()
+        || (table.decl.sync.is_some() && !table.decl.online_only) { return None; }
+    fn alternatives<'a>(value: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a Expr>) {
+        match value {
+            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => { alternatives(left, out); alternatives(right, out); }
+            other => out.push(other),
+        }
+    }
+    let mut disjuncts = Vec::new(); alternatives(rule, &mut disjuncts);
+    let admitted = disjuncts.iter().any(|value| {
+        let Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } = value else { return false };
+        let Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } = value.as_ref() else { return false };
+        if op != "not" || probe.range.is_some() || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some()
+            || !probe.seeks.is_empty() || probe.keys.len() != 1 || intent::column(&probe.keys[0].value) != Some("id") { return false; }
+        let Some(target) = tables.get(&probe.table) else { return false };
+        let Some(columns) = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, table, probe) else { return false };
+        target.indexes.iter().any(|index| target.total_unique(index) && index.columns == columns)
+    });
+    if !admitted { return None; }
+    let mut candidate = requests.clone(); let errors = candidate.error_count();
+    let mut system = read.clone(); candidate.read(tables, &mut system, false);
+    let mut lane = read.clone(); candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane);
+    let mut lanes = Vec::with_capacity(2); let mut order = None;
+    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, system, SYSTEM_LANE, None, None, None)?;
+    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane, VACANCY_LANE, None, None, None)?;
+    if candidate.error_count() != errors { return None; }
+    *requests = candidate;
+    Some(MergeExpr { view_order: Vec::new(), table: read.table.clone(), matching: None, filter: None,
+        index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor.clone(), lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
+}

     match rule {
         Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "and" || op == "!=" => true,

 }
 
+/// An authored equality may already be the right authorization scope while a
+/// named order index leaves it as a residual filter. Print the two exact edits
+/// together: the compatible declaration and the same query selecting it.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — equality-filter advice is mechanically coherent even when it remains advisory.
+fn equality_filter_index_rewrite(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan,
+    unpinned: &[String], leading: &[String], declaration: Option<&str>) -> Option<String> {
+    use snapback4_core::ir::{BinaryOp, Expr};
+    fn pins(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
+        match expr {
+            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left, right } => pins(left, column) || pins(right, column),
+            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left, right } => field(left, column) && independent(right)
+                || field(right, column) && independent(left),
+            _ => false,
+        }
+    }
+    fn field(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
+        matches!(expr, Expr::Field { base, name } if name == column
+            && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter"))
+    }
+    fn independent(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
+        match expr {
+            Expr::Field { base, .. } => !matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter") && independent(base),
+            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => independent(left) && independent(right),
+            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => independent(value),
+            Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().all(independent),
+            Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().all(independent),
+            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().all(independent),
+            Expr::Get(_) | Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Scan(_) | Expr::Merge(_) | Expr::Block { .. } => false,
+            _ => true,
+        }
+    }
+    let filter = scan.filter.as_deref()?;
+    if unpinned.is_empty() || leading.len() > 8 || !unpinned.iter().all(|column| pins(filter, column)) { return None; }
+    let title = |value: &str| { let mut chars = value.chars(); chars.next()
+        .map(|first| first.to_uppercase().collect::<String>() + chars.as_str()).unwrap_or_default() };
+    let stem = leading.iter().filter(|column| column.as_str() != "id").map(|column| title(column)).collect::<String>();
+    if stem.is_empty() { return None; }
+    let mut name = format!("by{stem}");
+    let mut suffix = 2;
+    while table.indexes.contains_key(&name) {
+        name = format!("by{stem}{suffix}"); suffix += 1;
+    }
+    let declaration = declaration?;
+    let old = format!(" by {}", scan.index);
+    if !declaration.contains(&old) { return None; }
+    let rewritten = declaration.replacen(&old, &format!(" by {name}"), 1);
+    Some(format!("on table {} add `by {name}: {}`; use the same index in the rewritten query:\n```quarry\n{rewritten}\n```", scan.table, leading.join(", ")))
+}
+
 fn matching_merge_rewrite(schema: &Schema, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan, table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table) -> Option<String> {
     use snapback4_core::{ir::IndexKeyExpr, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}};
     if !scan.prefix.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_))) { return None; }
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
index 198e9455c7..c33f192dd7 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
@@ -75,12 +75,24 @@ fn range_width(domain: &A) -> Option<u64> {
 // A lane-dependent predicate needs the eager binding snapshot. Plain row
 // predicates can keep the same head strategy as explicitly written lanes.
 pub(super) fn filter_uses_lane(value: &A, name: &str) -> bool {
+    let read_uses_lane = |read: &ast::ReadExpr| {
+        read.keys.iter().any(|key| filter_uses_lane(&key.value, name))
+            || read.seeks.iter().any(|seek| filter_uses_lane(&seek.value, name))
+            || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| filter_uses_lane(filter, name))
+            || read.matching.as_deref().is_some_and(|matching| filter_uses_lane(matching, name))
+            || read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref())
+                .is_some_and(|cursor| filter_uses_lane(cursor, name))
+            || read.computed.values().any(|value| filter_uses_lane(value, name))
+    };
     match value {
         A::Name { name: used, .. } => used == name,
         A::Focal { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => false,
         A::Member { base, .. } | A::Unary { value: base, .. } => filter_uses_lane(base, name),
         A::Binary { left, right, .. } => filter_uses_lane(left, name) || filter_uses_lane(right, name),
         A::Call { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| filter_uses_lane(arg, name)),
+        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — an independent fixed point guard does
+        // not need a snapshot of the varying membership row.
+        A::Exists { read, .. } | A::Created { read, .. } | A::Read(read) => read_uses_lane(read),
         _ => true,
     }
 }

+
+#[test]
+fn two_principal_status_scope_and_fixed_plus_per_row_grants_are_complete() {
+    let escorts = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable escorts:\n  student: principal\n  volunteerOne: principal ?\n  volunteerTwo: principal ?\n  status: enum('requested', 'assigned', 'arrived', 'walking', 'completed')\n  at: int\n  read <- .student = viewer or exists roles[viewer] or ((.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking'))\n  online only\nquery walks(c: cursor ?):\n  return escorts where (.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking') first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), escorts.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("assigned".into())), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", id("alice")), ("status", Value::String("walking".into())), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("hidden")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("completed".into())), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "walks", "alice"), ["a", "b"]);
+
+    let grants = "table chapterAccess:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:\n  version: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique version, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versions:\n  chapter: int\n  at: int\n  read <- exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer] and exists versionAccess[.id, viewer]\n  online only\nquery versionsOf(chapter: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return versions where .chapter = chapter first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), grants.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("chapterAccess".into(), object([("id", id("ca")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("versionAccess".into(), object([("id", id("va")), ("version", id("a")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let args = |chapter| BTreeMap::from([("chapter".into(), Value::Int(chapter)), ("c".into(), Value::Null)]);
+    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("alice", args(1))).unwrap();
+    assert!(r.complete, "{r:?}");
+    assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|row| row.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["a"]);
+    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("bob", args(1))).unwrap();
+    assert!(r.complete && r.data.as_array().unwrap().is_empty(), "{r:?}");
+
+    // A rule probe sees its target with system authority, while the residual
+    // query probe uses ordinary read authority.  Keep PAGE out unless those
+    // observations are equivalent for a timeless readable exact point.
+    for unsafe_grant in [
+        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  read <- deny", 1),
+        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  until: time ?\n  expire at .until\n  read <- .principal = viewer", 1),
+        grants.replacen("  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal", "  principal: principal\n  active: bool\n  unique chapter, principal", 1)
+            .replace("exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer]", "exists chapterAccess[chapter = .chapter, principal = viewer, active = true]"),
+    ] {
+        let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), unsafe_grant)]);
+        assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
+        assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", declined.notes);
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn equality_filter_note_prints_one_matching_index_and_query() {
+    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byCrewStarts"), "{note}");
+
+    let repaired = source.replace("  by byStart: starts, id", "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id")
+        .replace("first 240 by byStart", "first 240 by byCrewStarts");
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn exact_vacancy_pages_publish_only_rule_visible_exhaustion() {
+    let source = "table artists:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable designs:\n  oneOfKind: bool\n  at: int\n  read <- exists artists[viewer] or not .oneOfKind or not exists holds[design = .id] or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]\n  online only\ntable holds:\n  design: designs\n  owner: principal\n  expiresAt: time ?\n  unique design\n  read <- .owner = viewer or exists artists[viewer]\n  expire at .expiresAt\n  online only\nquery available(c: cursor ?):\n  return designs first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("artists".into(), object([("id", id("artist")), ("principal", id("art"))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(false)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("c")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
+        ("holds".into(), object([("id", id("h")), ("design", id("c")), ("owner", id("alice")), ("expiresAt", Value::Null)])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "bob"), ["a", "b"]);
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "alice"), ["a", "b", "c"]);
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "art"), ["a", "b", "c"]);
+
+    let non_point = source.replace("  design: designs\n", "  design: designs ?\n")
+        .replace(" or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]", "");
+    let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), non_point)]);
+    assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
+    assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"));
+}

# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

+fn page5_plans_require_branch_pages_v4_from_the_device() {

+  helper: principal
+  status: text <=10
+  at: int
+  read <- (.owner = viewer or .helper = viewer) and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'active')
+  online only
+query page(c: cursor ?):
+  return items first 1 by .at after c
+";
+    for (name, source) in [("fixed", fixed), ("vacancy", vacancy)] {
+        let (_, backend) = compile(source);
+        assert!(backend.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"), "{name}");
+        let required = snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&backend.schema.public_projection());
+        let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+        old.version = "0.4.28-page4".into();
+        old.capabilities.retain(|capability| capability != "branch-pages-v4");
+        let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION", "{name}");
+        assert!(refusal.message.contains("branch-pages-v4"), "{name}: {refusal:?}");
+    }
+    let (_, product_backend) = compile(product);
+    assert!(!product_backend.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"),
+        "bounded products reuse the existing branch validator");
+}

 /// The scenarios are written once over this object-safe view of a host:
 /// open, close, and the calls a test makes through one JSON dispatcher.
 pub trait HostDyn {

+
+/// PAGE5's three new planner families are generated independently so their
+/// evidence does not depend on the broader widening stream selecting a page.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — bounded products, fixed+varying grants and exact vacancy all receive generated cases.
+pub fn page5(seed: u64, shape: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    let mut rng = Rng::new(seed ^ 0x5041_4745_355f_454e ^ shape);
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1 + rng.pick(3) as u32,
+        descending: rng.pick(2) == 0, filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.program.lets.clear();
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["id".into()]); case.program.named_page_order = None;
+    case.program.principal_arg = None; case.program.residual = None; case.program.guard = None;
+    case.program.search_term = None; case.program.membership_named_reordered = false;
+    case.program.boolean_grant_normalized = false; case.program.kids = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None; case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    let bucket = case.args["bucket"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
+    match shape % 3 {
+        0 => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::ProductScope;
+            case.program.domain = Some("(.owner = viewer or .helper = viewer) and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'active')".into());
+            let items = &mut case.program.tables[0];
+            if !items.columns.iter().any(|column| column.name == "helper") { items.columns.push(column("helper", Type::Principal)); }
+            if !items.columns.iter().any(|column| column.name == "status") { items.columns.push(column("status", Type::Text)); }
+            for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+                row["helper"] = json!(if rng.pick(2) == 0 { "alice" } else { "bob" });
+                row["status"] = json!(["open", "active", "closed"][rng.pick(3) as usize]);
+            }}
+        }
+        1 => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::FixedPerRow;
+            case.program.domain = Some(".bucket = bucket".into());
+            case.program.order = Some(vec!["score".into()]);
+            id_membership(&mut case);
+            let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+            access.rule = Rule::Member; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Online;
+            access.expire = None; access.not_before = None; access.caps.clear();
+            let roles = Table { name: "roles".into(), columns: vec![column("bucket", Type::Int), column("principal", Type::Principal)],
+                keys: vec![("byBucketPrincipal".into(), vec!["bucket".into(), "principal".into()], true)], caps: vec![],
+                rule: Rule::Member, residency: Residency::Online, effects: None, expire: None, not_before: None };
+            case.program.tables.push(roles);
+            if rng.pick(4) != 0 { case.rows.push(("roles".into(), json!({"id":format!("role-{seed}"), "bucket":bucket, "principal":case.principal}))); }
+        }
+        _ => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Vacancy;
+            case.program.domain = Some("true".into());
+            id_membership(&mut case);
+            let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+            access.rule = Rule::Member; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Online;
+            access.expire = None; access.not_before = None; access.caps.clear();
+            access.keys.retain(|(name, _, _)| name == "byItem");
+            access.keys.iter_mut().for_each(|(_, columns, unique)| { *columns = vec!["item".into()]; *unique = true; });
+            let mut items = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+            case.rows.retain(|(table, row)| table != "access" || items.insert(row["item"].to_string()));
+        }
+    }
+    case
+}
+
+pub(crate) fn page5_features(case: &Case) -> Vec<String> {
+    let family = match case.program.tables[0].rule {
+        Rule::ProductScope => Some("product-scope"),
+        Rule::FixedPerRow => Some("fixed-per-row"),
+        Rule::Vacancy => Some("vacancy"),
+        _ => None,
+    };
+    family.map(|name| vec![format!("page5/{name}")]).unwrap_or_default()
+}

+
+#[test]
+fn page5_generated_families_compile_and_compare_natively() {
+    let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+    for shape in 0..3 {
+        for seed in 0..8 {
+            let case = snapback4_difftest::generate::page5(seed, shape);
+            seen.extend(snapback4_difftest::coverage::features(&case).into_iter().filter(|f| f.starts_with("page5/")));
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("shape {shape} seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let (verdict, executions) = oracle::repeat(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(verdict, Verdict::Pass) && executions >= 2,
+                "shape {shape} seed {seed}: {verdict:?}\n{source}");
+        }
+    }
+    assert_eq!(seen, ["page5/fixed-per-row", "page5/product-scope", "page5/vacancy"].map(String::from).into());
+}

+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — simulator execution covers the PAGE5 fixed
+// point guard beside a per-row membership and exact vacancy exhaustion.
+#[test]
+fn page5_product_fixed_membership_and_vacancy_publish_complete_pages() {
+    let product = bound("table items:
+  owner: principal
+  helper: principal
+  status: text <=10
+  at: int
+  read <- (.owner = viewer or .helper = viewer) and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'active')
+  online only
+query page():
+  return items first 10 by .at
+");
+    assert!(!product.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(product.schema, product.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([
+        ("items".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("a")), ("owner", id("alice")), ("helper", id("bob")), ("status", Value::String("open".into())), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("items".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("b")), ("owner", id("bob")), ("helper", id("alice")), ("status", Value::String("active".into())), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+        ("items".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("c")), ("owner", id("alice")), ("helper", id("bob")), ("status", Value::String("closed".into())), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let result = engine.query("page", context("alice", &[])).unwrap();
+    assert!(result.complete && result.next.is_none(), "{result:?}");
+    let ids: Vec<_> = result.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|row| row.field("id").unwrap().clone()).collect();
+    assert_eq!(ids, [id("a"), id("b")]);
+
+    let fixed = bound("table chapters:
+  bucket: int
+  principal: principal
+  unique bucket, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table access:
+  item: items
+  principal: principal
+  unique item, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table items:
+  bucket: int
+  at: int
+  read <- exists chapters[.bucket, viewer] and exists access[.id, viewer]
+  online only
+query page(bucket: int, c: cursor ?):
+  return items where .bucket = bucket first 1 by .at after c
+");
+    assert!(fixed.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(fixed.schema, fixed.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([
+        ("chapters".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("chapter")), ("bucket", Value::Int(1)), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("access".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("access")), ("item", id("visible")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("items".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("visible")), ("bucket", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("items".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("hidden")), ("bucket", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let result = engine.query("page", context("alice", &[("bucket", Value::Int(1)), ("c", Value::Null)])).unwrap();
+    assert!(result.complete && result.next.is_none(), "{result:?}");
+    assert_eq!(result.data.as_array().unwrap()[0].field("id").unwrap(), &id("visible"));
+
+    let vacancy = bound("table holds:
+  item: designs
+  owner: principal
+  unique item
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table designs:
+  owner: principal
+  read <- not exists holds[item = .id] or .owner = viewer
+  online only
+query available(c: cursor ?):
+  return designs first 1 by .id after c
+");
+    assert!(vacancy.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut engine = Engine::memory(vacancy.schema, vacancy.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    engine.seed([
+        ("holds".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("hold")), ("item", id("held")), ("owner", id("alice"))])),
+        ("designs".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("free")), ("owner", id("alice"))])),
+        ("designs".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([("id", id("held")), ("owner", id("alice"))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let result = engine.query("available", context("bob", &[("c", Value::Null)])).unwrap();
+    assert!(result.complete && result.next.is_none(), "{result:?}");
+    assert_eq!(result.data.as_array().unwrap()[0].field("id").unwrap(), &id("free"));
+}
 // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — only live memberships may synthesize PAGE target lanes.
 #[test]
 fn owner_or_membership_page_ignores_an_expired_unswept_witness() {
diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Eval.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Eval.lean
index 88bb1a8455..f53c348985 100644
--- a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Eval.lean
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Eval.lean
@@ -568,6 +568,35 @@ def laneFits (order : List String) (eqs pins : List (String × Expr)) : Bool :=
 def laneDecided (td : TableDecl) (order : List String) (eqs pins : List (String × Expr)) : Bool :=
   laneFits order eqs pins && decidedPins td ((eqs ++ pins).filter fun p => p.1 != "id" && !order.contains p.1)
 
+/-- PAGE5's fixed half of a fixed-plus-varying grant: after substituting the
+page pins, this is one timeless, required exact point whose ordinary read
+rule discloses the same answer to the caller. -/
+def fixedPointGuard (sch : Schema) (outer : TableDecl)
+    (pins : List (String × Expr)) : Rule → Bool
+  | r@(.probe t point key) => match sch.table? t with
+    | some source =>
+      let columns := key.map (·.1)
+      key.length == point.length
+        && point.eraseDups.length == point.length
+        && point.all columns.contains
+        && (point == ["id"] || source.uniques.contains point)
+        && point.all (fun name => name == "id" || source.cols.any (fun col => col.name == name && !col.nullable))
+        && source.notBefore.isNone && source.expire.isNone
+        && r.usableGuard sch outer pins
+    | none => false
+  | _ => false
+
+/-- PAGE5's varying half has exactly one outer-row key component and otherwise
+uses PAGE's existing readable, total membership witness. -/
+def varyingPointGrant (sch : Schema) (pins : List (String × Expr))
+    (order : List String) : Rule → Bool
+  | .probe t point key => membershipEligible sch t point key
+      && (membershipColumns key).any fun pair =>
+        !pins.any (·.1 == pair.2)
+          && (!order.contains pair.2 || pair.2 == "id" && order == ["id"])
+          && laneFits order [(pair.2, .viewer)] pins
+  | _ => false
+
 /-- Each positive alternative either has a prefix-decided rule, its own
 readable guard, or one exact membership witness with every remaining
 conjunct true by its pins. -/
@@ -593,6 +622,9 @@ def compoundDecided (sch : Schema) (td : TableDecl) (pins : List (String × Expr
                   !ps.any (·.1 == pair.2) && (!order.contains pair.2 || pair.2 == "id" && order == ["id"])
                   && laneFits order ((pair.2, .viewer) :: extra) pins
               | _ => false
+            | [a, b] =>
+                (fixedPointGuard sch td ps a && varyingPointGrant sch ps order b)
+                  || (fixedPointGuard sch td ps b && varyingPointGrant sch ps order a)
             | _ => false)
 
 
@@ -621,6 +653,17 @@ def scopedWitnessDecided (sch : Schema) (td : TableDecl)
               && laneFits order [(pair.2, .viewer)] pins
     | _ => false
 
+/-- PAGE5's explicit vacancy: a top-level negated probe of one required
+exact point by the focal row's intrinsic id. The native lane still checks the
+whole read rule per row; this predicate governs only its exhaustion verdict. -/
+def vacancyDecided (sch : Schema) (td : TableDecl) : Bool :=
+  td.read.disjuncts.any fun r => match r with
+    | .not (.probe t [point] [(key, .col .self "id")]) =>
+        point == key && (sch.table? t).any fun source =>
+          source.uniques.contains [point]
+            && (source.cols.find? (·.name == point)).all (!·.nullable)
+    | _ => false
+
 /-- The pins the single-index planner keeps: the page's equalities and bare
 boolean conjuncts, except `id` and an order column other than the first,
 which stay seeks (`Requests::read`; GPT round 3, finding 3). -/
@@ -705,7 +748,8 @@ def branchDecided (sch : Schema) (t : String) (pins : List (String × Expr))
       | none => false
     let case4 := guards && !reservedMembership && !pinnedMembership && alts.isNone && compoundDecided sch td pins order
     let witnessScope := guards && pins.all (fun p => p.2.total) && scopedWitnessDecided sch td pins fs order
-    (total && (case1 || case2)) || (readFree && (case3 || case4)) || witnessScope
+    let vacancy := guards && pins.isEmpty && fs.isEmpty && vacancyDecided sch td
+    (total && (case1 || case2)) || (readFree && (case3 || case4)) || witnessScope || vacancy
 
 def keyValue (k : Key) : Value := .arr (k.map .s)
 
diff --git a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Examples.lean b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Examples.lean
index 71d780efc7..008fdfc8e7 100644
--- a/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Examples.lean
+++ b/snapback4/lean/Quarry/Examples.lean
@@ -931,6 +931,68 @@ def page4Examples : List Example := [
     expected := r#"{"kind":"answer","data":[],"complete":true,"next":null}"# }
 ]
 
+def page5Product : Schema := { tables := [
+  { name := "items", cols := [c "owner", c "helper", c "status", c "at"]
+    read := .and
+      (.or (.eq (self "owner") .viewer) (.eq (self "helper") .viewer))
+      (.or (.eq (self "status") (.lit (T "open"))) (.eq (self "status") (.lit (T "active")))) }] }
+
+def page5FixedPerRow : Schema := { tables := [
+  { name := "roles", cols := [c "bucket", c "principal"], uniques := [["bucket", "principal"]]

+/// Every sibling of a varying membership witness must already hold. Preserve

+fn fixed_rule_holds<'a>(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
+    outer_name: &str, outer_table: &Table, pinned: &impl Fn(&str) -> Option<&'a Expr>,
+    pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
+    if rule_true(rule, outer_name, outer_table, pinned) { return true; }
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::And(parts) => parts.iter().all(|part|
+            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
+        RuleExpr::Or(parts) => parts.iter().any(|part|
+            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
+        _ => fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, filter, rule, pins),
+    }
+}
+
+/// A generated membership lane may retain prefix-fixed point grants

+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — fixed grants may guard one varying per-row grant.
 fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
     pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
     if let Expr::Binary { op: crate::ir::BinaryOp::And, left, right } = filter {
         return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
             || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
     }
+    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
+    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
+    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+            condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = body.as_slice()
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice()
+        && else_body.is_empty() && condition == name && assigned == name
+        && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(result) if result == name) {
+        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
+            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
     let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
     equalities.is_empty() && target.through.is_none() && target.not_before.is_none() && !target.is_expiring()
         && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.iter().all(|part| part.column()
             .and_then(|column| target.columns.get(column)).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
