         && read.key.iter().zip(key).all(|(expression, term)| rule_term_expression(term, expression, pins))
+        && point_read_is_visible(schema, read)
+}
+
+/// A rule probe uses system authority, but the retained residual point uses
+/// ordinary caller authority. Re-prove that the generated, pin-substituted
+/// point is visible instead of asking about the unsubstituted authored terms.
+fn point_read_is_visible(schema: &Schema, read: &crate::ir::Read) -> bool {
+    let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false };
+    let Some(index) = table.index(&read.index) else { return false };
+    if !index.is_plain() || index.components.len() != read.key.len() { return false; }
+    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&read.key).find_map(|(part, value)|
+        (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then_some(value));
+    visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true
 }

+/// A membership PAGE with two independent `or` filters stays on the ordinary
+/// incomplete scan. The native membership planner distributes at most one
+/// such filter; the independent model must not silently discard the second.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — undistributed query alternatives are outside the constructive PAGE fragment.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_fallback(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::OwnerOrWitness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };

+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .flag = false)".into());
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear();
+    case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear();
+    case
+}

 }
+
+#[test]
+fn membership_page_with_two_filter_alternatives_stays_incomplete_in_lean_and_native() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        let case = snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_fallback(seed);
+        let source = case.program.source();
+        execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+        let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+        let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+        assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], false, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+        let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+        assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+        if have_model {
+            let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+        }
+    }
+}

 #[test]
 fn equality_filter_note_prints_one_matching_index_and_query() {
-    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
+    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable logs:\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  public\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  ignored = logs first 1 by byStart\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
     let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
     assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
     let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
     assert!(note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
-    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byCrewStarts"), "{note}");
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("ignored = logs first 1 by byStart"), "{note}");
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches[] first 240 by byCrewStarts where .crew = viewer"), "{note}");
 
-    let repaired = source.replace("  by byStart: starts, id", "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id")
-        .replace("first 240 by byStart", "first 240 by byCrewStarts");
+    let rewritten = note.rewrite.split("```quarry\n").nth(1).and_then(|rest| rest.split("\n```").next()).unwrap();
+    let query = source.find("query myWatches():").unwrap();
+    let repaired = format!("{}{}\n", source[..query].replace("  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer",
+        "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer"), rewritten);
     let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
     assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
     assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);

   947	/// An authored equality may already be the right authorization scope while a
   948	/// named order index leaves it as a residual filter. Print the two exact edits
   949	/// together: the compatible declaration and the same query selecting it.
   950	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — equality-filter advice is mechanically coherent even when it remains advisory.
   951	fn equality_filter_index_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table,
   952	    scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan, unpinned: &[String], leading: &[String],
   953	    declaration_site: &crate::Site, read_site: Option<&crate::Site>) -> Option<String> {
   954	    use snapback4_core::ir::{BinaryOp, Expr};
   955	    fn pins(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
   956	        match expr {
   957	            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left, right } => pins(left, column) || pins(right, column),
   958	            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left, right } => field(left, column) && independent(right)
   959	                || field(right, column) && independent(left),
   960	            _ => false,
   961	        }
   962	    }
   963	    fn field(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
   964	        matches!(expr, Expr::Field { base, name } if name == column
   965	            && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter"))
   966	    }
   967	    fn independent(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
   968	        match expr {
   969	            Expr::Field { base, .. } => !matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter") && independent(base),
   970	            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => independent(left) && independent(right),
   971	            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => independent(value),
   972	            Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().all(independent),
   973	            Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().all(independent),
   974	            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().all(independent),
   975	            Expr::Get(_) | Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Scan(_) | Expr::Merge(_) | Expr::Block { .. } => false,
   976	            _ => true,
   977	        }
   978	    }
   979	    let filter = scan.filter.as_deref()?;
   980	    if unpinned.is_empty() || leading.len() > 8 || !unpinned.iter().all(|column| pins(filter, column)) { return None; }
   981	    let title = |value: &str| { let mut chars = value.chars(); chars.next()
   982	        .map(|first| first.to_uppercase().collect::<String>() + chars.as_str()).unwrap_or_default() };
   983	    let stem = leading.iter().filter(|column| column.as_str() != "id").map(|column| title(column)).collect::<String>();
   984	    if stem.is_empty() { return None; }
   985	    let mut name = format!("by{stem}");
   986	    let mut suffix = 2;
   987	    while table.indexes.contains_key(&name) {
   988	        name = format!("by{stem}{suffix}"); suffix += 1;
   989	    }
   990	    let declaration = declaration?;
   991	    let old = format!(" by {}", scan.index);
   992	    if !declaration.contains(&old) { return None; }
   993	    let rewritten = declaration.replacen(&old, &format!(" by {name}"), 1);
   994	    Some(format!("on table {} add `by {name}: {}`; use the same index in the rewritten query:\n```quarry\n{rewritten}\n```", scan.table, leading.join(", ")))
   995	}
   996	
   997	fn matching_merge_rewrite(schema: &Schema, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan, table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table) -> Option<String> {
   998	    use snapback4_core::{ir::IndexKeyExpr, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}};
   999	    if !scan.prefix.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_))) { return None; }
  1000	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — a membership conjunct supplies lanes when their scope decides the whole rule.
  1001	    fn conjuncts(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
  1002	        match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => terms.iter().flat_map(conjuncts).collect(), other => vec![other] }
  1003	    }
  1004	    conjuncts(&table.rules.read).into_iter().find_map(|rule| {
  1005	        let RuleExpr::Exists { table: members, index: membership_index, key, .. } = rule else { return None; };
  1006	        let membership = schema.table(members)?;
  1007	        let columns = membership.index(membership_index)?.columns();
  1008	        let principal = key.iter().position(|term| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Viewer))?;
  1009	        let scope: Vec<_> = key.iter().filter_map(|term| if let RuleTerm::Field(field) = term { Some(field.clone()) } else { None }).collect();
  1010	        let deciding = snapback4_core::engine::rules::deciding_fields(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read);
  1011	        if !deciding.iter().all(|field| scope.contains(field)) { return None; }
  1012	        let fields = table.index(&scan.index)?.word_component()?.1;
  1013	        let (_, words_index) = table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| index.word_component().is_some_and(|(position, text)| index.columns()[..position] == scope && text == fields))?;
  1014	        let _ = membership.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| index.is_plain() && index.columns().first() == columns.get(principal) && index.columns().last().is_some_and(|column| column == "id"))?;
  1015	        let order = words_index.columns().into_iter().skip(scope.len()).map(|column| format!(".{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
  1016	        Some(format!("{} matching q where {} last {} by {order} after c — one verdict per scope; complete while every head opens and the page fits in one read; or read the page on the device", scan.table, table.rules.read.quarry(), scan.take.maximum()))
  1017	    })
  1018	}
  1019	
  1020	/// Every merge a program's body evaluates.
  1021	pub use snapback4_core::search_plan::merges;

  1023	// Admission rewrites remove the rejected page bound, retaining its exact pins.
  1024	fn whole_group_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, source: &str, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan) -> String {
  1025	    let (doc, errors) = crate::parse_document("rewrite.q", &format!("query group():\n  return {source}\n"));
  1026	    if errors.is_empty() && let Some(crate::ast::Stmt::Return { value: crate::ast::Expr::Read(read), .. }) = doc.programs.first().and_then(|program| program.body.first()) {
  1027	        let mut read = read.clone();
  1028	        intent::Requests::default().read(&catalog.tables, &mut read, false);
  1029	        let index = catalog.tables.get(&scan.table).and_then(|info| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| index.name == scan.index));
  1030	        let pins = read.keys.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(i, key)| {
  1031	            let column = key.column.as_ref().or_else(|| index.and_then(|index| index.columns.get(i)))?;
  1032	            Some(format!(".{column} = {}", crate::print::expression(&key.value)))
  1033	        }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
  1034	        return if pins.is_empty() { read.table } else { format!("{} where {}", read.table, pins.join(" and ")) };
  1035	    }
  1036	    source.into()
  1037	}

+/// A top-level `not exists point[.id]` explicitly makes vacancy observable.
+/// One server lane checks the original rule for every candidate and may report
+/// exhaustion only after the physical range ends. It carries no ordinary
+/// filter because rule probes use wider system visibility.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact vacancy is the sole non-uniform exhaustion lane.
+pub(super) fn vacancy(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests,
+    read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
+    let range = read.range.as_ref()?;
+    let table = tables.get(&read.table)?;
+    let rule = branches::read_rule(table)?;
+    if read.filter.is_some() || read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some()
+        || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some()
+        || (table.decl.sync.is_some() && !table.decl.online_only) { return None; }
+    fn alternatives<'a>(value: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a Expr>) {
+        match value {
+            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => { alternatives(left, out); alternatives(right, out); }
+            other => out.push(other),
+        }
+    }
+    let mut disjuncts = Vec::new(); alternatives(rule, &mut disjuncts);
+    let admitted = disjuncts.iter().any(|value| {
+        let Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } = value else { return false };
+        let Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } = value.as_ref() else { return false };
+        if op != "not" || probe.range.is_some() || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some()
+            || !probe.seeks.is_empty() || probe.keys.len() != 1 || intent::column(&probe.keys[0].value) != Some("id") { return false; }
+        let Some(target) = tables.get(&probe.table) else { return false };
+        let Some(columns) = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, table, probe) else { return false };
+        target.indexes.iter().any(|index| target.total_unique(index) && index.columns == columns)
+    });
+    if !admitted { return None; }
+    let mut candidate = requests.clone(); let errors = candidate.error_count();
+    let mut system = read.clone(); candidate.read(tables, &mut system, false);
+    let mut lane = read.clone(); candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane);
+    let mut lanes = Vec::with_capacity(2); let mut order = None;
+    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, system, SYSTEM_LANE, None, None, None)?;
+    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane, VACANCY_LANE, None, None, None)?;
+    if candidate.error_count() != errors { return None; }
+    *requests = candidate;
+    Some(MergeExpr { view_order: Vec::new(), table: read.table.clone(), matching: None, filter: None,
+        index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor.clone(), lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
+}

         Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "and" || op == "!=" => true,

+interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
+that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
+executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
+lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
+classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.

+simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
+limitations rather than completeness claims.

+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — older devices refuse PAGE5's validator
+// interpretation instead of executing fixed residual or vacancy lanes as v3.
+#[test]
+fn page5_plans_require_branch_pages_v4_from_the_device() {
+    let vacancy = "table holds:
+  item: designs
+  owner: principal
+  unique item
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table designs:
+  owner: principal
+  read <- not exists holds[item = .id] or .owner = viewer
+  online only
+query available(c: cursor ?):
+  return designs first 1 by .id after c
+";
+    let fixed = "table roles:
+  bucket: int
+  principal: principal
+  unique bucket, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table access:
+  item: items
+  principal: principal
+  unique item, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table items:
+  bucket: int
+  at: int
+  read <- exists roles[.bucket, viewer] and exists access[.id, viewer]
+  online only
+query page(bucket: int, c: cursor ?):
+  return items where .bucket = bucket first 1 by .at after c
+";
+    let product = "table items:
+  owner: principal
+  helper: principal
+  status: text <=10
+  at: int
+  read <- (.owner = viewer or .helper = viewer) and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'active')
+  online only
+query page(c: cursor ?):
+  return items first 1 by .at after c
+";
+    for (name, source) in [("fixed", fixed), ("vacancy", vacancy)] {
+        let (_, backend) = compile(source);
+        assert!(backend.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"), "{name}");
+        let required = snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&backend.schema.public_projection());
+        let mut old = snapback4_core::device_protocol::DeviceEngine::current();
+        old.version = "0.4.28-page4".into();
+        old.capabilities.retain(|capability| capability != "branch-pages-v4");
+        let refusal = snapback4_core::device_protocol::check(Some(&old), &required).unwrap_err();
+        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_DEVICE_VERSION", "{name}");
+        assert!(refusal.message.contains("branch-pages-v4"), "{name}: {refusal:?}");
+    }
+    let (_, product_backend) = compile(product);
+    assert!(!product_backend.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"),
+        "bounded products reuse the existing branch validator");
+}
+

+/// PAGE5's three new planner families are generated independently so their
+/// evidence does not depend on the broader widening stream selecting a page.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — bounded products, fixed+varying grants and exact vacancy all receive generated cases.
+pub fn page5(seed: u64, shape: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    let mut rng = Rng::new(seed ^ 0x5041_4745_355f_454e ^ shape);
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1 + rng.pick(3) as u32,
+        descending: rng.pick(2) == 0, filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.program.lets.clear();
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["id".into()]); case.program.named_page_order = None;
+    case.program.principal_arg = None; case.program.residual = None; case.program.guard = None;
+    case.program.search_term = None; case.program.membership_named_reordered = false;
+    case.program.boolean_grant_normalized = false; case.program.kids = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None; case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    let bucket = case.args["bucket"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
+    match shape % 3 {
+        0 => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::ProductScope;
+            case.program.domain = Some("(.owner = viewer or .helper = viewer) and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'active')".into());
+            let items = &mut case.program.tables[0];
+            if !items.columns.iter().any(|column| column.name == "helper") { items.columns.push(column("helper", Type::Principal)); }
+            if !items.columns.iter().any(|column| column.name == "status") { items.columns.push(column("status", Type::Text)); }
+            for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+                row["helper"] = json!(if rng.pick(2) == 0 { "alice" } else { "bob" });
+                row["status"] = json!(["open", "active", "closed"][rng.pick(3) as usize]);
+            }}
+        }
+        1 => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::FixedPerRow;
+            case.program.domain = Some(".bucket = bucket".into());
+            case.program.order = Some(vec!["score".into()]);
+            id_membership(&mut case);
+            let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+            access.rule = Rule::Member; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Online;
+            access.expire = None; access.not_before = None; access.caps.clear();
+            let roles = Table { name: "roles".into(), columns: vec![column("bucket", Type::Int), column("principal", Type::Principal)],
+                keys: vec![("byBucketPrincipal".into(), vec!["bucket".into(), "principal".into()], true)], caps: vec![],
+                rule: Rule::Member, residency: Residency::Online, effects: None, expire: None, not_before: None };
+            case.program.tables.push(roles);
+            if rng.pick(4) != 0 { case.rows.push(("roles".into(), json!({"id":format!("role-{seed}"), "bucket":bucket, "principal":case.principal}))); }
+        }
+        _ => {
+            case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Vacancy;
+            case.program.domain = Some("true".into());
+            id_membership(&mut case);
+            let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+            access.rule = Rule::Member; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Online;
+            access.expire = None; access.not_before = None; access.caps.clear();
+            access.keys.retain(|(name, _, _)| name == "byItem");
+            access.keys.iter_mut().for_each(|(_, columns, unique)| { *columns = vec!["item".into()]; *unique = true; });
+            let mut items = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+            case.rows.retain(|(table, row)| table != "access" || items.insert(row["item"].to_string()));
+        }
+    }
+    case
+}
+
+pub(crate) fn page5_features(case: &Case) -> Vec<String> {
+    let family = match case.program.tables[0].rule {
+        Rule::ProductScope => Some("product-scope"),
+        Rule::FixedPerRow => Some("fixed-per-row"),
+        Rule::Vacancy => Some("vacancy"),
+        _ => None,
+    };
+    family.map(|name| vec![format!("page5/{name}")]).unwrap_or_default()
+}

+
+#[test]
+fn page5_generated_families_compile_and_compare_natively() {
+    let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
+    for shape in 0..3 {
+        for seed in 0..8 {
+            let case = snapback4_difftest::generate::page5(seed, shape);
+            seen.extend(snapback4_difftest::coverage::features(&case).into_iter().filter(|f| f.starts_with("page5/")));
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("shape {shape} seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let (verdict, executions) = oracle::repeat(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(verdict, Verdict::Pass) && executions >= 2,
+                "shape {shape} seed {seed}: {verdict:?}\n{source}");
+        }
+    }
+    assert_eq!(seen, ["page5/fixed-per-row", "page5/product-scope", "page5/vacancy"].map(String::from).into());
+}

   705	## 14. PAGE: capped owner-or-membership pages
   706	
   707	PAGE resolves Q6 for the common rule
   708	
   709	```quarry
   710	read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
   711	```
   712	
   713	when the membership lookup names an exact unique key and the membership table
   714	declares a finite cap whose grouping columns are fixed by the viewer or other
   715	row-independent expressions. More formally, the read rule's top-level
   716	disjunction has at least one principal-party equality and at least one such
   717	membership probe, and every disjunct is one of those two forms. Every
   718	qualifying probe has one row-dependent exact-key component naming an ordinary
   719	target column (not `id`) that the page has not already pinned, and otherwise
   720	fixed components; its cap group is wholly among the fixed components and its
   721	read rule is decided true by them. The §2–§3 exclusions still apply: total
   722	page predicates, shared order and total cursor suffix, portable keys and
   723	labels, at most eight physical components in both source and target indexes,
   724	and fallback without speculative indexes. The planner first lowers
   725	bare booleans and negated booleans to equality pins, as it does for every
   726	other intent plan. It emits the party lanes, one descriptor per membership
   727	probe, and one system lane. Their static descriptor count remains at most
   728	eight; each declared membership cap bounds its descriptor's dynamic fanout
   729	separately and may itself be greater than eight.
   730	
   731	The dynamic descriptor scans the membership range to completion before the
   732	page may emit a row. Each distinct parent in that result opens a target range
   733	pinned to that parent. The membership row is the witness for the rule branch,
   734	so target rows in that range need no second membership probe; their ordinary
   735	target coverage, horizon and expiry checks still apply. The owner and parent
   736	ranges merge in the requested order and `id`, deduplicate overlaps, and share
   737	one version-4/5 position. Source discovery and every opened target scan use
   738	the query's one store transaction/snapshot, so a membership cannot be revoked
   739	between proving a lane and reading it. The system lane reads the whole
   740	original range and suppresses every other lane for a system context.
   741	
   742	The cursor descriptor includes the static lanes and the membership source's
   743	table and index. Its scope includes the evaluated static pins and the source
   744	range's fixed viewer/argument pins. It deliberately does not digest the set of
   745	parents returned by that range: membership rows, like target rows, may change
   746	between requests. This is ordinary non-snapshot pagination. The next request
   747	answers completeness for its current remaining range strictly after the
   748	shared position; it makes no claim about rows at or before that position. A
   749	new membership therefore contributes only currently readable rows after the
   750	cursor, and a removed membership contributes no further rows. Schema or
   751	fixed-principal changes still restart because they change the descriptor or
   752	scope.
   753	
   754	The backend carries `branch-membership-v1` in addition to `branch-merge-v1`.
   755	An older device understands the static branch cursor but not the mandatory
   756	discovery-before-output step, so it refuses this backend instead of attempting
   757	the eager merge. Handshake negotiation uses the ordinary schema-capability
   758	path.
   759	
   760	Membership discovery is an all-or-nothing admission step. Source rows,
   761	distinct-parent retention and every dynamic lane descriptor charge the
   762	ordinary examined, step and heap meters. A meter cut, cap
   763	latch, unknown presence, incomplete source coverage or source horizon before
   764	the source range is known complete returns an empty incomplete page and no
   765	continuation. Minting either a normal continuation or a skip continuation at
   766	that point could omit an undiscovered parent whose first row sorts before an
   767	already emitted owner row. Once discovery finishes, ordinary per-range meter
   768	cuts use the §6 frontier and may return a safe continuation.
   769	
   770	The heap ledger applies to the whole membership merge, including owner and
   771	system lane buffers as well as dynamic target buffers. It reserves source
   772	distinct sets, evaluated prefixes and bounds, merge-position and heap slots,
   773	deduplication, the emitted-page vector, frontier decode, and cursor/page-record
   774	scratch before allocation. A source distinct key is first sized by the
   775	allocation-free canonical JSON writer, then charged and encoded into one
   776	bounded buffer of exactly that requested capacity; escaped parent text cannot
   777	cause geometric growth after admission. Compiled scans remain shared; dynamic
   778	descriptors borrow one compiled target scan rather than cloning its filter tree
   779	per parent.
   780	
   781	On a device the membership source and every opened target range retain their
   782	normal per-scan coverage decisions. PAGE is an explicit exception to §7's
   783	blanket synced-union exclusion for this proved source shape: it has a
   784	system-only lane rather than a probe-grant lane, so acquired membership and
   785	target union groups can use the same plan and produce the same complete page
   786	as the server. An online or unacquired source makes discovery unknown and
   787	returns the empty incomplete answer above. An unheld or horizon-incomplete
   788	target range makes the result incomplete and triggers the ordinary server
   789	fallback; no partial device result is marked complete. The plan does not
   790	treat a merely uniform denial as an empty membership set: binding validates
   791	that the source pins decide its read rule true. Expiry from membership
   792	discovery joins target expiry at the existing minimum.
   793	
   794	The planner and Lean classifier mirror point-probe resolution before accepting
   795	the branch. A positional probe selects the one declared unique whose ordered
   796	column types accept its arguments; equal-arity keys with different types do not
   797	make it ambiguous, while multiple matching keys still refuse the positional
   798	spelling. Named probe columns are duplicate-free and contain every column of a
   799	duplicate-free declared unique, additional columns are fixed equalities, and a
   800	cap covers the fixed discovery prefix. The descriptor bound counts each party
   801	column once, matching runtime party-lane deduplication while retaining every
   802	distinct membership branch. Its soundness lemma says every emitted
   803	party or parent lane row is admitted under the branch-witness assumption; the
   804	logical theorem does not prove that runtime discovery establishes that
   805	witness. Its coverage lemma splits every admitted row into a party witness or
   806	membership witness. Differential cases include
   807	caps above eight and overlap. Runtime meter regressions cover discovery cuts;
   808	simulator regressions include revocation between pages and missing acquisition;
   809	server and both device stores exercise the same shared-cursor walk.
   810	
   811	The implicit `byId` also proves an exact named probe containing `id`; any
   812	additional named components remain required residual equalities. This does not
   813	invent a multi-part positional key: positional probes still resolve through
   814	their typed declared unique, apart from the ordinary single-ID spelling.
   815	
   816	A fixed named component may remain an exact equality in the discovery scan's
   817	residual filter rather than its capped prefix, as in
   818	`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]`. PAGE accepts that
   819	component only when the equality is a necessary conjunct over the source row;
   820	an alternative or computed implication is not witness evidence. The retained
   821	filter therefore restricts discovery to `m1`, and a row with the same parent
   822	and principal under another id cannot open a target lane. The planner checks
   823	that every fixed probe component survives as a prefix pin or such a residual
   824	before committing candidate indexes, and the bound-IR validator repeats the
   825	same check without copying the filter. A denial, missing witness, expired
   826	witness, incomplete source horizon or unsupported residual cannot expose an
   827	unproved target lane. Uniform denial and unsupported residuals keep the
   828	ordinary fallback without speculative PAGE indexes. A missing or expired
   829	witness opens no lane under complete discovery, so the owner merge remains
   830	complete; an incomplete source horizon returns the empty incomplete result
   831	described above.
   832	
   833	Fixed scalar equalities compare their canonical values across the rule and
   834	expression representations. In particular, unary minus applied to an integer
   835	literal is canonicalized to that negative integer before PAGE validates the
   836	residual and before candidate indexes are committed. Thus `slot = -1` is the
   837	same necessary residual in planning, bound validation and the Lean model; it
   838	does not cause a late fallback that leaves speculative indexes behind.
   839	
   840	The intrinsic source `id` is always such a residual, even when fixed. It stays
   841	in the exact logical witness and its equality must hold, but it is not a
   842	discovery-prefix component and cannot satisfy the discovery cap. Thus `cap by
   843	id` alone keeps the ordinary incomplete scan; a separate cap over fixed
   844	discovery columns such as `principal` is required for PAGE.
   845	
   846	An optional discovered parent may be `null` only when the exact point probe
   847	identifies the source row without that parent component. For example,
   848	`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` addresses `m1`
   849	through `byId` and checks `parent = null` as a residual equality, so a matching
   850	source row opens one null-parent target range. Discovery uses its ordinary
   851	null-covering physical index, including the stored row-id suffix, and applies
   852	the fixed residual before distinct-parent deduplication. If the varying parent
   853	is instead a component of the probe's nullable unique key, a null argument has
   854	the ordinary point result of no row and cannot open a PAGE lane. The same is
   855	true when any other component of that point key is null. This rule neither
   856	turns nullable uniques into total uniques nor changes their storage or
   857	collision semantics.
   858	
   859	A varying source field may be the discovered parent even when it is not the
   860	next physical index component, provided the fixed discovery prefix covers a
   861	plain total unique index. Such a prefix selects at most one source row, so PAGE
   862	may project any field from that row: the implicit `id` in
   863	`groups[id = .parent, principal = viewer]`, or an ordinary `parent` in
   864	`groups[parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` under `unique principal`.
   865	The total unique may be a proper subset of the probe's fixed source columns:
   866	`unique principal` also admits
   867	`groups[parent = .parent, slot = 0, principal = viewer]`. PAGE keeps `slot =
   868	0` as an exact residual on the selected source row; a missing row, `slot =
   869	null`, or any other slot does not open a target range. The provisional wider
   870	discovery index is discarded before requests are committed. When several
   871	covered total uniques exist, PAGE chooses only among points whose prefix
   872	independently proves the source read rule and the discovery cap; an earlier
   873	`unique a` cannot displace an eligible `unique principal` by moving
   874	`principal = viewer` into the residual.
   875	The projected field may be optional under the null-witness rule above. The
   876	source cap, readability, complete-discovery, resource and horizon checks still
   877	apply. Every component of the one-row unique itself must be non-optional; a
   878	nullable unique does not supply this proof and declines before PAGE indexes
   879	are committed. In all other cases the parent remains the next physical
   880	source-index component.
   881	
   882	The Lean surface gate prices the same physical PAGE candidate before invoking
   883	the logical oracle. Equality-prefix columns use the planner's canonical sorted
   884	order followed by the exact scan-order suffix; the permutation allowed for an
   885	extremum is not available to a PAGE discovery scan. A total declared unique in
   886	that exact order may omit the trailing physical `id`, because its components
   887	already identify the row. If two declared indexes both match the candidate and
   888	the author selected neither, native optional planning declines the ambiguous
   889	PAGE candidate and the emitter reports `page-membership-index-selection`
   890	instead of asking an index-free logical model to choose one. Component, byte or
   891	label overflow reports `page-membership-key-bound`; a reused nullable unique
   892	that cannot prove the varying-`id` one-row case reports
   893	`page-membership-source-unique`. Each stops before the index-free model.
   894	
   895	## 15. PAGE2: ordinary exact memberships, row ids and parent owners
   896	
   897	Wave 10 showed that §14's cap requirement excluded the ordinary membership
   898	schema it was meant to serve:
   899	
   900	```quarry
   901	table members:
   902	  parent: parents
   903	  principal: principal
   904	  unique parent, principal
   905	  read <- .principal = viewer
   906	
   907	table documents:
   908	  owner: principal
   909	  parent: parents
   910	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
   911	```
   912	
   913	`unique parent, principal` proves that the rule's point probe has at most one
   914	witness for one document and caller. It does **not** prove that a principal has
   915	only finitely many membership rows: fixing `principal` leaves arbitrarily many
   916	`parent` values. PAGE2 therefore does not call this source range statically
   917	capped. It admits the exact total-unique witness and runs the inverted
   918	`members where .principal = viewer` discovery under the ordinary read meter.
   919	The §14 discovery fence already gives the required failure semantics: until
   920	the source range is known complete, no owner or membership target row is
   921	emitted; an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or discovery `steps`
   922	cut, unknown device coverage or incomplete horizon returns an empty
   923	`complete: false` page with no continuation. `heap_bytes` exhaustion retains
   924	the existing `E_BOUND` read refusal. Every
   925	distinct value, evaluated prefix and dynamic lane is sized and charged through
   926	§14's heap ledger before allocation, so an uncapped population cannot allocate
   927	past the request ceiling. Retrying a read-meter cut may complete under a larger
   928	budget or smaller population. It cannot skip an undiscovered lane or turn
   929	partial discovery into a negative authorization fact.
   930	
   931	This admission proves a safe plan, not completion for every population size.
   932	For a caller with `K` memberships, discovery and target-head work grow with
   933	`K`; once a read ceiling is crossed the fenced result is empty and incomplete,
   934	so a sufficiently large audience can be temporarily unavailable under the
   935	ordinary budget. A cap is therefore optional for correctness and remains the
   936	product's availability bound. If an app must guarantee that the page completes
   937	within a chosen budget, the writer that grants memberships must enforce a cap
   938	on the fixed enrollment group (for example `principal`). A writer allowed to
   939	grant arbitrary memberships controls `K`; point uniqueness alone does not.
   940	
   941	A declared cap is a separate static proof only when all of its grouping
   942	columns are fixed by the discovery scan and null exemption cannot apply. Any
   943	maximum is admissible: a cap above the request's examined ceiling may still
   944	cut safely under the preceding rule. A cap grouped by `parent` does not bound a
   945	scan that fixes only `principal`; a cap grouped by `principal` does. A fully
   946	pinned total unique remains the one-row case. Planner, bound-IR validator and
   947	Lean emitter use these same alternatives:
   948	
   949	1. the rule probe selects the intrinsic `id` or one declared plain unique,
   950	   and every other probe component is an exact necessary equality;
   951	2. either that selected point is total, the discovery itself is a fully pinned
   952	   total unique, or a declared cap covers the fixed discovery prefix;
   953	3. the fixed pins decide the source read rule true, and all existing index,
   954	   key, label, descriptor, total-predicate and shared-position checks pass.
   955	
   956	A nullable original unique is not a total point proof. It can still use PAGE
   957	when discovery pins every column of a separate total unique, or an applicable
   958	cap bounds its discovery group. For example, nullable `unique parent, principal`
   959	remains the positional rule probe while required `unique principal` bounds the
   960	inverted `principal = viewer` discovery to one row. This does not rewrite the
   961	original probe or its null semantics: a null in that selected point retains the
   962	ordinary result of no witness. If a cap is the only missing condition,
   963	`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` gives the exact declaration to add, using the source's
   964	fixed non-null columns: for the usual shape, `cap 50 by principal`; with no
   965	fixed group, `cap 50`. The note also says that a cap is a stored write
   966	constraint: adding it can refuse existing adoption or later inserts once that
   967	group holds 50 live rows (and physically retained rows where cap validation
   968	requires them), so the author should add it only when it is a real product
   969	limit. The note is withheld when a cap cannot repair the
   970	shape, including multiple row-dependent components, a hidden source, a
   971	non-exact or ambiguous probe, a multi-hop target, incompatible ordering, or a
   972	physical key bound. It must not prescribe code that still cannot plan.
   973	
   974	The varying target column may now be the target row's own `id`:
   975	
   976	```quarry
   977	read <- .owner = viewer or exists shares[document = .id, principal = viewer]
   978	```
   979	
   980	Discovery yields document ids, and each dynamic target lane pins `id`. A
   981	one-row id range is trivially ordered, but it still reports the page's shared
   982	logical position, for example `.at, id`: the row supplies `.at`, and `id` is
   983	the final tie-breaker just as in every other lane. All lanes therefore share
   984	the existing version-4/5 cursor and frontier rules. Before any row is emitted,
   985	the engine opens every discovered id lane and reads its one possible head. If
   986	an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or `steps` cut leaves even one
   987	unfinished lane without a head/`through`, §6's frontier is absent and the page
   988	is empty, incomplete and has no continuation: no returned row can sort ahead
   989	of an unread point. Only
   990	after every point lane is exhausted or has a head can the heap emit the true
   991	`.at, id` prefix. Heap exhaustion remains `E_BOUND` as above. The implicit
   992	`byId` is a valid exact source point too, which admits the parent-owner form
   993	
   994	```quarry
   995	read <- .owner = viewer or exists offers[id = .offer, baker = viewer]
   996	```
   997	
   998	when `baker = viewer` decides the offer source readable. Its inverted source
   999	scan uses or synthesizes the plain `baker, id` discovery order (within the
  1000	existing width, byte, label and ambiguity checks), discovers the caller's
  1001	offer ids in the same transaction snapshot, and target lanes pin `.offer`; it
  1002	does not require `cap N by baker`. Both forms preserve the rule's exact
  1003	witness, snapshot, expiry, device-coverage and meter requirements.
  1004	
  1005	Discovery and target scans use their ordinary lifetime filter. A source row
  1006	before `not before` is not a witness and opens no lane; a future target row is
  1007	not a lane head and cannot be emitted early. PAGE contributes no lifetime
  1008	bypass or separate clock. Source and target opening commits invalidate the
  1009	ordinary watched table revisions; expiry joins the existing minimum deadline.
  1010	No opening timestamp is exported in the cursor or `nextExpiry`.
  1011	
  1012	The schema requires `branch-membership-v2` when a membership operation uses a
  1013	metered discovery source or pins the target row's intrinsic `id`. The optional
  1014	operation fact preserves that classification across schema serialization;
  1015	the existing `branch-membership-v1` remains required for every membership
  1016	merge, so capped §14 plans remain v1-compatible. A v1-only engine or device
  1017	refuses a PAGE2 backend by the ordinary capability handshake instead of
  1018	applying its older admission validator or downgrading the plan to an eager
  1019	merge.
  1020	
  1021	The Lean classifier admits row-`id` targets and the same three source proofs:
  1022	a total original point probe, fully pinned total-unique discovery, or an
  1023	applicable cap. The fully pinned proof is checked on the discovery pins and
  1024	does not replace a nullable original positional probe. Its logical `ownerMembership_sound` and
  1025	`ownerMembership_cover` theorems remain about the exact rule leaves; executable
  1026	examples add uncapped composite membership, row-id membership and parent-owner
  1027	pages. Native, Lean and device differential generators draw the same forms,
  1028	including source populations that cross the examined meter and must return the
  1029	empty incomplete discovery result, and id populations whose target-head phase
  1030	cuts before every lane opens. The language, simulator and both device stores
  1031	walk each form through one shared cursor and test overlap de-duplication.
  1032	
  1033	### PAGE2 implementation and qualification — 2026-10-13
  1034	
  1035	§15 is implemented at `24a644c511fc5a6888597af9335d894790eaeb97`. Both
  1036	independent [Muse](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.muse.md) and
  1037	[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.codex.md) mark this amendment READY,
  1038	using the same frozen §15 and implementation checkpoint. This records the
  1039	amendment's implementation/readiness; it leaves the parent RFC's legacy Review
  1040	status intact and makes no author-acceptance or publication claim.
  1041	
  1042	The concrete fully pinned discovery-unique parity case passes after its old
  1043	model control fails completion. The intrinsic-id competing-unique case passes
  1044	after its old planner control has no merge. The discovery step sweep passes
  1045	after positive old-code ceilings 2/3/6/8/11/12/14/15 produce E_BOUND. Final
  1046	checks: language page2 membership 5/5 and branch merge 57/57; Lean difftests 32/32;
  1047	smoke 17/17; Lean 58-theorem standard-axiom audit, 71/71 examples, 7/7 fixtures;
  1048	core discovery 1/1 and meter 9/9; simulator membership 8/8; device branch merge 12/12
  1049	on Memory/SQLite. Heap and non-step/pending errors retain their semantics.
  1050	
  1051	Final 400-case native/device/Lean campaigns have no new signatures. Native
  1052	20,619 and device 13,114 executions have zero disagreements; Lean 1,072
  1053	executions has only three instances of its two pre-existing classified
  1054	pagination signatures. Additional generated native seed 715 and Lean 551

  1056	uncovered device source remains unknown/incomplete. Full results and commands

  1058	
  1059	All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile. `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` falls from 20 notes in 13
  1060	schemas to 18 notes in 11 schemas ; 001 incomingRequests and 037 myChildren clear, and named
  1061	even 002/098 each clear their sole note. The remaining hidden-source, pure
  1062	membership, role-grant and compound shapes are explicitly excluded; no claim
  1063	is made that the raw 20-note corpus or all membership-shaped notes met the
  1064	large-majority target. [Final survey](../snapback4/reports/20261013-page2/final-schema-survey.md)
  1065	binds source/binary/output hashes at the final semantic checkpoint.
  1066	
  1067	The clean Linux web artifact at `10d27f62cc` is 2,305,135 B raw / 703,255 B gzip /
  1068	531,512 B brotli, budget 703,755 B = measured gzip + 500 B. Serializer-family and
  1069	forbidden-absence audits and locked wasm/client builds pass. No push.
  1070	
  1071	## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans
  1072	
  1073	Commissioned 2026-10-13; implemented locally. Muse and fresh Codex both
  1074	READY in round 5 at `65d505289f`; verbatim reviews and frozen brief are
  1075	`llp/reviews/3000.036-page3.r5.{muse,codex,brief}.md`.
  1076	PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
  1077	party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
  1078	and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather
  1079	than asking builders to merge lists themselves. It changes no read authority,
  1080	replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.
  1081	
  1082	**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
  1083	rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
  1084	server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
  1085	original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
  1086	`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
  1087	table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
  1088	only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
  1089	pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
  1090	applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
  1091	A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
  1092	position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.
  1093	
  1094	**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
  1095	cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
  1096	rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
  1097	probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
  1098	others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
  1099	single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
  1100	LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
  1101	and acquisition invariants.
  1102	
  1103	**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
  1104	`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
  1105	range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
  1106	readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
  1107	positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
  1108	no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
  1109	empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.
  1110	
  1111	**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
  1112	alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
  1113	of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
  1114	witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
  1115	conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
  1116	be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
  1117	a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
  1118	probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
  1119	admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
  1120	or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.
  1121	
  1122	A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
  1123	pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
  1124	a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
  1125	covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
  1126	lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
  1127	there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
  1128	predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
  1129	Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
  1130	empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
  1131	and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.
  1132	
  1133	A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
  1134	its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
  1135	nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
  1136	wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
  1137	owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and
  1138	parent-teacher/status rules preserve each status condition when opening lanes.
  1139	
  1140	**Readable static predicates.** A `where` disjunction may repeat a readable
  1141	static point, such as `.gleaner = viewer or exists coordinators[viewer]`.
  1142	Every point-key expression must be an infallible, read-free name or literal
  1143	(including canonical signed numeric literals); arithmetic, assertions, calls
  1144	and member reads keep the original scan and short circuit. The point must be
  1145	a total unique with fixed terms, readable by those pins or
  1146	its identical self-witness rule, without a lifetime or read-through boundary.
  1147	It remains in the original predicate for every caller, including the system.
  1148	This extension belongs to static party/where branch merges only. Membership
  1149	discovery and compound-rule expansion retain their read-free total filter
  1150	requirement, mirrored separately by model cases 3 and 4. The physical point
  1151	can cut even though its logical predicate is total. The
  1152	branch frontier therefore excludes an interrupted filter candidate; it may
  1153	continue only from an earlier fully evaluated candidate, so retry cannot
  1154	skip the interrupted row. A source its holders cannot read keeps its scan.
  1155	
  1156	A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
  1157	on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
  1158	not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.
  1159	
  1160	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v2` fences guarded descriptors,
  1161	held-union merge behavior and fully pinned point-lane admission; it is derived
  1162	from bound plans, serialized as an optional operation fact, and older engines
  1163	refuse it through the ordinary handshake. Existing descriptor bytes remain
  1164	unchanged when none of these new lane types is present. Membership-only plans
  1165	retain the existing membership capability rules. The Lean surface model
  1166	removes its party requirement, and models the admitted positive rule ranges.
  1167	Enums refuse the entire model module with the existing explicit `type-enum`
  1168	reason before any program is declared supported. Compound where-branch
  1169	composition, nullable constant pins and physically nonportable/ambiguous
  1170	compound plans have their own named emitter exclusions. Native static
  1171	predicate keys that are arguments or signed expression nodes use the named
  1172	`page-static-key-expression` model boundary until their separate readability
  1173	classifier is extended; they are unsupported, never counted as oracle passes;
  1174	unsupported fragments remain explicitly named rather than accepted by the
  1175	oracle. Its logical leaf soundness/coverage proof includes pure membership.
  1176	
  1177	**Hidden-source boundary and exact repairs.** PAGE3 does not scan private
  1178	parent rows whose owner cannot necessarily read them. No cap/index alone
  1179	repairs that absence proof. Copy the parent's immutable owner onto the child,
  1180	keep that copy and its parent reference immutable, keep the parent non-expiring,
  1181	and retain the reference with `on delete restrict`, and check the copy against the parent in the child's
  1182	insert rule (and in every trusted seed/backfill). Replace the parent-owner
  1183	read-rule leaf with the copied owner comparison. The copy preserves authority
  1184	only while the parent owner is immutable; a mutable owner needs transactional
  1185	propagation or the original rule. Existing data must be backfilled before
  1186	adoption. The diagnostic names the actual table, reference and owner column
  1187	and supplies exact source edits. It never recommends making parents public.
  1188	
  1189	**PAGE3 qualification and review dispositions (2026-10-14).** Round 1
  1190	repaired null-exempt bounds, parent reference/lifetime preservation, outer
  1191	conjunction guards and capability scope; round 2 repaired fallible static keys,
  1192	block-lowered probe fencing and defensive model bounds. Round 3 named static
  1193	argument/signed-key model exclusions, with zero-oracle Unsupported regressions.
  1194	Round 4 separated readable-static model filters from the read-free gates for
  1195	membership/compound plans, with actual native/model data and completeness
  1196	comparisons. Round 5's two independent reviewers marked the same revision
  1197	READY. No unsupported model case is counted as a pass.
  1198	
  1199	All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile: 18 notes in 11 schemas
  1200	become 2 in one (both hidden parents). Applying the exact checked copy repair
  1201	to a further copied corpus gives 0 in all 50. Per-shape counts, input/output
  1202	hashes and source edits are in `snapback4/reports/20261013-page3/`. Final
  1203	400-case campaigns have 22,277 native and 12,686 device executions without
  1204	disagreements; Lean's 1,108 executions retain only the three existing C2
  1205	pagination occurrences, with no new signature. Lean audits 59 theorems with
  1206	standard axioms only, runs 74 examples and seven fixtures. Focused language,
  1207	model, simulator and both device-store suites pass.
  1208	
  1209	The clean locked Linux web artifact at `f11a5e72cd` is 2,310,393 B raw /
  1210	704,662 B gzip / 532,356 B brotli; budget 705,162 B = measured gzip + 500 B.
  1211	Family/absence audits and wasm/client builds pass. Later edits affect only
  1212	language/model/tests, preserving these device bytes. No push or publication.
  1213	
  1214	## 17. PAGE4: complete multi-audience pages and exact scopes
  1215	
  1216	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-11 fix #11. This amendment extends the
  1217	existing bounded planner. No read authority, replicated audience, runtime
  1218	ceiling or cursor codec changes. Query unions containing a public status
  1219	are separate from union *sync*: a public sync branch remains excluded.
  1220	
  1221	**Mixed positive alternatives.** Positive distribution also applies to a
  1222	simple disjunction mixing party equality, public/status equality, exact
  1223	membership and readable static grants. Each alternative retains every
  1224	conjunct and the complete original query predicate. Equality lanes,
  1225	guarded ranges and exact discovered membership lanes share the existing
  1226	ordered id-tied merge, with duplicate positions emitted once. At most seven
  1227	alternatives plus the system range fit. Speculative source/target indexes
  1228	are retained only when the bound validator accepts the whole plan.
  1229	
  1230	Named plain page indexes normalize to their declared column order only in
  1231	a speculative merge candidate. A declined candidate keeps its original read.
  1232	An exact owner/member rule whose varying component is necessarily pinned
  1233	can use the existing party/guard merge; alternative-only pins do not qualify.
  1234	
  1235	**Exact where witnesses.** A positive whole `where` conjunct identical to a
  1236	whole read-rule disjunct can define an exact narrower audience: for example
  1237	`posts where exists authors[.id, viewer] first 20 by .at after c`. Discover
  1238	that readable witness under §§14–16's total point and complete-discovery
  1239	proofs. Other audiences cannot contribute rows to W. Every other conjunct
  1240	must be read-free and total. The original predicate remains in admission
  1241	validation; the system descriptor retains the authored witness, because
  1242	system bypasses R but never W. Its prefix also binds the fixed target pins
  1243	into the shared cursor scope. A hidden source keeps the original scan.
  1244	
  1245	**Pinned components of exact witnesses.** A positive alternative may pin
  1246	one component of a multi-column rule probe. Substitute only a necessary
  1247	infallible fixed equality, leaving one varying source component; the core
  1248	validator independently checks the original field term against that exact
  1249	target prefix. Example: `.manager = viewer and exists reporting[report =
  1250	.report, manager = .manager]` discovers reporting rows with manager=viewer,
  1251	then their report values. The other party's alternative inverts the other
  1252	component. Extra named point equalities survive. No arithmetic, missing
  1253	pin, second varying component or hidden source is treated as proof.
  1254	
  1255	**Parent snapshots.** Selecting `s.id` or a stored scalar field of a required
  1256	point-read row image is a fixed lexical value, not a new row read. In a
  1257	standalone page's predicate (returned or assigned), the compiler binds those
  1258	selections once before planning. Optional rows, reference hops, private/maintained fields,
  1259	rebindings and joined paths keep the original predicate. A snapshot does
  1260	not prove that a child's copied band/manager equals its parent's field;
  1261	source repairs must say when their extra pins narrow the authored scope.
  1262	
  1263	**Membership word search.** The existing compact ordinary server search may
  1264	derive its exact two-component total membership audience on an online-only
  1265	table too. Provision the reverse readable source index and scoped words
  1266	index in one checked speculative candidate. Synced targets retain their
  1267	same exact audience requirement. The device program and sync holdings do
  1268	not acquire a predicate; empty needles retain the original browse arm.
  1269	Readability, non-expiring source, portable keys, opening meter refusal,
  1270	word-head merge and original cursor binding remain LLP 3000.028 §3.3's.
  1271	
  1272	**Honest incomplete forms.** A fallback note distinguishes an analyzer
  1273	limitation (the current admitted ranges cannot prove this positive shape)
  1274	from a truly partial read (its range/source cannot establish absence under
  1275	its declared authority). It names the exact complete line when one can be
  1276	verified; scope-narrowing pins say that they narrow the answer. A hidden
  1277	parent retains PAGE3's checked copy/backfill repair. No cap/index-only
  1278	suggestion is offered for an unreadable witness, and no copied relationship
  1279	is assumed from a successful parent read alone.
  1280	
  1281	**Readable negative static points and finite enums.** A negated static
  1282	probe is a page guard only when its selected key is total unique, its source
  1283	is readable at that key and has no maintained-state, read lifetime or read-through boundary.
  1284	Its absence is then an observable authorized fact. A hidden negative probe
  1285	still cannot open a complete range. Required enum inequality against a
  1286	literal expands to its exact remaining variants; optional enum inequality
  1287	against a non-null value stays residual. No varying negative source is
  1288	inverted. This completes the renovation selectionsFor page (its unpaged budget aggregation
  1289	retains an analyzer limitation) without
  1290	turning timed privacy into an unsupported absence claim.
  1291	
  1292	A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
  1293	on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
  1294	not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.
  1295	
  1296	**Compatibility and model.** Existing descriptor bytes and semantics serve
  1297	mixed unions and exact where witnesses. `branch-pages-v3` fences pinned multi-column witnesses, readable negative
  1298	static guarded/covering ranges and the compiler-owned `RequireUncapped`
  1299	primitive before an older validator or decoder can encounter them. A nullable
  1300	row assertion in a query checks the read meter after evaluating its point;
  1301	a cut raises the internal E_BOUND that the query boundary reports as an
  1302	unavailable incomplete answer, instead of inventing the author's absence error.
  1303	Ordinary absent/hidden rows still use the authored error, and mutation
  1304	assertions retain their transactional policy.
  1305	The independent Lean surface classifier must reproduce positive mixed
  1306	ranges, pinned witness substitution and exact where scope. Static snapshot
  1307	binding is an explicit syntax normalization, like where-guard lowering.
  1308	Word search stays a named unsupported model fragment; it must execute zero
  1309	Lean oracle cases, while native/device tests cover its existing words engine.
  1310	The direct-grant soundness theorem remains positive: negative-grant scalar
  1311	absence/aggregation, fallible-filter fallback and descriptor overflow have the
  1312	explicit `page-negative-grant-fallback` model boundary, including negative
  1313	probes nested in conjunctions and made static by prefix pins, and overflow of
  1314	distributed alternatives inside conjunctions, with zero oracle
  1315	executions. Standard bounded negative-grant pages use the independent range
  1316	classifier and are exercised against the model. No unsupported case is counted
  1317	as a pass. Fresh Muse and Codex reviews both mark semantic revision
  1318	`193f94ea9da7a046f4ce6fe76d7b527e1686ef90` READY. See the
  1319	[verbatim paired reviews and provenance](reviews/3000.036-page4.provenance.md).
  1320	Qualification is recorded separately in the PAGE4 workstream report.
  1321	
  1322	Model fidelity: lexical witness pins use an opaque classifier-only parameter
  1323	term, preserving variable identity instead of converting observed argument
  1324	values to literals or viewer proofs. The oracle rejects that term in authored
  1325	rule JSON; executable authority always evaluates the original rule. Named
  1326	plain orders normalize only within the independent unfiltered mixed-merge
  1327	fragment. Filtered/one-party named candidates outside that fragment (including declined
  1328	native candidates) have the explicit
  1329	`page-named-index-fallback` boundary and execute zero oracle cases. No such excluded candidate is claimed to be a native failure. This
  1330	retains native fallback semantics instead of claiming a complete owner pin
  1331	on an authored fixed index. Both review counterexamples have regressions;
  1332	the generator includes argument-valued pinned pairs.
  1333	
  1334	Delivered-history integration: an acquired device history is an audience in
  1335	its own right under LLP 3000.007 §18.2, even after live membership expires.
  1336	A membership merge on such a target selects its existing whole-range system
  1337	descriptor on the device without changing the actual viewer. It retains the
  1338	authored W, acquisition, lifetime, retention, meters and shared cursor scope;
  1339	only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
  1340	`branch-pages-v3` fences this device interpretation. Existing expiry/paged
  1341	snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
  1342	P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
  1343	correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.
  1344	
  1345	## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies
  1346	
  1347	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
  1348	planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
  1349	argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
  1350	query plan.
  1351	
  1352	**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
  1353	alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
  1354	classifies the resulting lanes.
  1355	For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
  1356	alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
  1357	the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
  1358	inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
  1359	negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
  1360	analyzer-limitation fallback.
  1361	
  1362	This does not erase query predicates that the membership PAGE planner cannot
  1363	distribute. It admits no query-filter alternative or one bounded positive
  1364	alternative set. Two independent `or` filters stay on the ordinary incomplete
  1365	scan even when each expression is total; the Lean classifier makes the same
  1366	decline instead of treating those predicates as absent.
  1367	
  1368	**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
  1369	be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
  1370	conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
  1371	the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
  1372	required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
  1373	pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
  1374	`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
  1375	into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
  1376	the core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
  1377	and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
  1378	authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
  1379	guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
  1380	fragment.
  1381	
  1382	**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
  1383	`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
  1384	plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
  1385	same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
  1386	candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
  1387	authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
  1388	therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
  1389	outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
  1390	around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
  1391	may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
  1392	rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
  1393	row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
  1394	target's absence is globally observable.
  1395	
  1396	**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
  1397	independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
  1398	using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
  1399	rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
  1400	its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
  1401	the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
  1402	When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
  1403	does not offer this rewrite.
  1404	
  1405	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
  1406	interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
  1407	that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
  1408	executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
  1409	lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
  1410	classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
  1411	Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
  1412	simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
  1413	limitations rather than completeness claims.
